Mr. Spinks analyzes the potential of the ultra-rightist
forces in Japan as follows:
What can be termed the ultra-rightist front today 1s
not a well-organized nationwide movement; and except for
several abortive attempts on Communist leaders' lives, has
not threatened the peace and order of the occupation.  It
includes some of the old rightist elements who have managed
to preserve some degree of corporate identity by participating
in non-political activities, such as poetry societies and
business concerns. A number of postwar rightist organiza-
tions have also re-established the traditional link between
the Japanese underworld (Kuromaku) and ultra-nationalism
FW
through affiliation with racketeers, bosses and black
market operators. But the continued operations of the
Kuromaku which is an ancient, well-established political
form should not be regarded as a sign of ultra-nationalistic
resurgence.
The appearance of an ultra-rightist front presents
two dangers. 1) It will intensify the left-right struggle
and nc matter which group emerges victorious, democracy
loses. 2) Although they are avowed mutual enemies, the
C
left and the right pursue the same conspiratorial and
terroristic methods and have the same contempt for popular
government. In fact so alike are the two totalitarian
extremes that their members seem to move with ease from
one extreme to the other.
The recent spate of criticism of an alleged fascist
2
revival in Japan - most of which is the work of well-known
leftists - strongly suggests that it 1s in part a Communist
diversionist tactic. The criticism follows closely the
increasingly anti-fascist tone which has characterized JCP
propaganda ever since the proposed revision of the National
Public Service Law, curbing strike and fraction activities
in almost half of Japan's organized labor movement.
This despatch well worth reading. Also suggest reading
first three pages of first enclosure.

TO
NA - Mr. Bishop
DATE: December
NA - Mr. Lory
FROM :
Communism in Japan.
SUBJECT:
Since the days when I was teaching at the Hokkaido
Imperial University and some of my students were arrested
for becoming infected with the Communist virus I have been
e
extremely interested in following (but not sympathetieally)
the course of Communism and radical movements within Japan.
Because of Hokkaido's proximity to Russian Sakhal
and Vladivostok, 1t was for several years before the wao
on the receiving end of perhaps more Communist propagande
B
than any other area in Japan proper. In my opinion,to
gether with other factors, much of the Communist strength
among students and certain labor circles in Hokkaido in
2
the late twenties and early thirties can be attributed tb
-1548
Hokkaido's geographical position. In view of this
Hokkaido today should be considered a valuable listening
post for detecting Communist infiltration into northern
Japan.
Writing in 1943 with this Hokkaido background, I was
denounced by the Communist publication "New Masses" for
stating that the economic program of the Communists was
similar to that of the extreme militarists in that they
both wanted to soak the rich and nationalize Japan's
industries.
In the light of the importance of following closely
today the Communist menace in Japan, I woula like to sug-
gest that NA prepare a bi-weekly or at some other interval,
a summary and analysis of Communist activities in Japan.
The material for this summary could be obtained from tele-
grams, airgrams, despatches, bulletins, etc. I would be
pleased to have your vomments regarding this suggestion.

The strike strategy of the Japan Commmmist Party brings
into play all the overt and eovert meehanisms of the Partyts
organization. The order for a umion strike erigimates with
the union's Central Executive Cemmittee and sifts dowm to the
lowest union level. The first step is to establish "straggle
committees" by mational, regional and prefeetural exeeutive
committees of the union. Sinee each executive committee has
a powerful Communist fraction, the struggle committees im turm
have their Commumist fractions.
The Japan Communist Party maintains liaison with Commmmmist
fractions in the various committees on a horisontal bmsis. with
this arrangement, fractions in the Central Struggle Committee
and in the Central Executive Committee of each member umion
receive their orders directly from the Central Committee of the
Japan Communist Party. Fractions in the regional struggle
committees and in the reglonal executive committees receive
their orders from the Regional Committees of the Japan Communist
Party, while fractions in the Prefectural Struggle Committees
and in the Prefectural Executive Committees receive their
orders from the Prefoctural Committees, of the Party.
There are no struggle committees at the local union chapter
level. The workers in the local unions who carry out the strike
itself are influenced and controlled by the Local Committees of
the

Strikes are not the only tools of the Communists that
undermine Japan's industrial recovery. Working through fraetiems
or cells, Communist Party members within the key industrial
unions often are successful in initiating and encouraging wide-

over purpose of disrupting production. Such practices combined
with Communist directed strikes retard the production of commodi-
ties essential for Japan's economic revival.(3)
